3

Low-incline bench press

Opener rehearsal week · 3 or more clean reps left

Use a 30° bench. Warm up with 20 kg × 8 for working weights of 25 kg or more. Add 30 kg × 5 when working at 35 kg or above.

  1. Set 18reps

Working weight. Repeat your last regular incline weight for eight only if you have three clean reps left. Otherwise use the next lighter 5 kg step. For example, a 55 kg training weight gives you 55 or 50 kg today. Save increases for the next training block.

Barbell steps and setup

20 · 25 · 30 · 35 · 40 · 45 · 50 · 55 · 60 · 65 · 70 kg. Continue in 5 kg steps. All weights include the bar. For work at 50 kg or above, an extra 1–3 warm-up reps one step below your working weight can help you judge today’s load.

Use safeties or a spotter. Keep your feet planted and your back comfortably supported. Use the same bench setting and controlled range each week. Rest 2–3 minutes between work sets.

The empty bar is 20 kg. If it exceeds today’s effort target, omit the incline set. If you have yet to learn this press, introduce it in a regular training week.

4

Accessories

Choose the weight by feel

  • Reverse pec deck2 × 12–201 left · rest 60–90 s. Open the arms without shrugging.
  • Incline or preacher curl2 × 8–121 left · rest 90 s. Incline with dumbbells, or preacher on the bench or the cable preacher. Upper arm still; lower under control.
  • Pallof press2 × 10–12 per siderest 60 s. Stand side-on to the cable, press straight out and resist turning.
  • Wrist curl2 × 12–201 left · rest 60 s. Forearms on a bench or the forearm cable machine, palms up; let the handle roll down to your fingers, then curl your wrists up.
  • Reverse wrist curl2 × 12–201 left · rest 60 s. Straight after the wrist curl, palms down; lift the backs of your hands, then lower slowly.

Monday 26 April · Week 26 · Test week

Squat test

1

Mobility warm-up

Before the first lift

  • Easy bike or walk3 min
  • Knee-to-wall ankle rocks2 × 10 per side
  • Adductor rock-backs× 10
  • Goblet squat hold at your depth2 × 20–30 s
  • Hip-hinge drill× 8
2

Back squat test

Up to three attempts

Warm-up in kg　20 × 8 · 55 × 5 · 80 × 3 · 100 × 2 · 110 × 1 · 120 × 1

  1. Opener130kg×1
  2. Second attempt140kg×1
  3. Third if earned145kg×1
Lighter125 → 130 → 135 kgWarm-up 20 × 8 · 55 × 5 · 80 × 3 · 95 × 2 · 110 × 1 · 120 × 1
Heavier140 → 145 → 150 kgWarm-up 20 × 8 · 60 × 5 · 85 × 3 · 105 × 2 · 120 × 1 · 130 × 1

Use up to three singles and rest 4–6 minutes between attempts. Choose an opener recently completed cleanly at RPE 8 or easier. If warm-ups feel heavy, use the lighter route. For 130 → 140 → 145 kg, take the 10 kg first increase only if 130 is clearly comfortable (about RPE 7–7.5 or easier) and your recent work supports 140. Otherwise, if another attempt is warranted, use 130 → 135 → 140 kg. Take the final 5 kg only after a clean second with clear reserve. The stretch route requires a recently repeatable opener. End the session after a miss, a grind, loss of your usual technique or concerning symptoms. Log the heaviest completed single.

Bench press test

Up to three attempts

Warm-up in kg　20 × 8 · 40 × 5 · 55 × 3 · 70 × 2 · 75 × 1 · 85 × 1

  1. Opener90kg×1
  2. Second attempt95kg×1
  3. Third if earned100kg×1
Lighter85 → 90 → 95 kgWarm-up 20 × 8 · 35 × 5 · 55 × 3 · 65 × 2 · 75 × 1 · 80 × 1
Heavier95 → 100 → 105 kgWarm-up 20 × 8 · 40 × 5 · 60 × 3 · 70 × 2 · 80 × 1 · 90 × 1

Use up to three singles and rest 4–6 minutes between attempts. Choose an opener recently completed cleanly at RPE 8 or easier. If warm-ups feel heavy, use the lighter route. 90 → 95 → 100 kg is the goal route. A 90 kg opener at RPE 9 ends the test. At RPE 8, consider 95 only if recent work supports it. Attempt 100 only after a clean 95 at about RPE 8–8.5 or easier with clear reserve. If 95 is a genuine RPE 9, finish there. Use 95 → 100 → 105 only when 95 is a reliably comfortable opener and 100 also leaves clear reserve. End the session after a miss, a grind, loss of your usual technique or concerning symptoms. Log the heaviest completed single.

Plan guide

How to read the printed weights, and everything around them.

The 26 weeks

One bar per week, coloured by phase. The outlined bar is the week chosen on the Plan page.

NovDecJanFebMarApr
  • Start · week 1Record squat depth and choose working weights from recent sets. Test strict pull-ups, dips and push-ups on Thursday.
  • Build · weeks 2–8Squat 4 × 5–6, sumo 3 × 4–5. Progress by completed reps. Flat and paused bench on Tuesday, volume bench and weighted dips on Saturday.
  • Easier week · week 9Reduce barbell work and repeat the Thursday bodyweight benchmarks.
  • Strength · weeks 10–16Squat 4 × 3–4 and sumo 3 × 3–4. Keep Tuesday flat and paused bench. Add one easy practice single per primary lift in weeks 12 and 16.
  • Easier week · week 17Reduce barbell work and repeat the Thursday bodyweight benchmarks.
  • Heavy · weeks 18–23Each primary lift gets one working single and back-offs. Earn each 5 kg increase on two comparable training weeks.
  • Opener rehearsal · week 24Rehearse familiar openers at RPE 7–8. Reduce back-offs and Thursday incline work.
  • Taper · week 25Use short, comfortable sets. Main examples: squat 105 kg for 2 × 2, bench 80 kg for 3 × 2, sumo 130 kg for 2 × 2.
  • Test week · week 26Squat Monday, bench Tuesday, recovery Wednesday, sumo Thursday, recovery Friday and bodyweight tests Saturday. Log results Sunday.

Progression and test targets

Work sets, checkpoints and attempts for all three lifts

Strength targets in kg
LiftReported bestFirst PRGoalStretch
Squat125130145150
Bench press9095100105
Sumo deadlift160165175180

What counts. A completed single at your logged standard is a measured result. Record a PR on the day you lift it, including in training. Your 110 kg × 8 squat suggests the reported 125 kg best may be conservative. Use the same depth for comparisons. Use the same unassisted bench touch and a fully reset sumo pull.

Weeks 1–8. Starting examples are squat 100 kg for 4 × 5, bench 70 kg for 6, 6, 5 plus paused 65 kg for 2 × 4, and sumo 120 kg for 3 × 4. After week 1, squat uses 4 × 5–6 at 100, 105, 110 or 115 kg. Sumo uses 3 × 4–5 at 120, 125, 130 or 135 kg. Use the easier starting examples as needed.

Squat and sumo progression. Start a new load at the bottom of its range. Complete the top reps on every set with at least two left on the last set at the same load twice. Add 5 kg on the next comparable main day and reset the reps. Hold with only one left, or reduce by 5 kg following a miss or grind. The examples show one possible pace. In week 1 only, you may add 5 kg next main session after completing all work with at least four clean reps left.

Weeks 10–16. Squat 4 × 3–4 at 110, 115, 120 or 125 kg. Sumo 3 × 3–4 at 135, 140, 145 or 150 kg. Flat bench uses 80 or 85 kg when earned plus paused bench at 75 or 80 kg. The Lighter rows help you enter either phase when the normal loads exceed your recent work.

Bench in the build and strength phases. On two regular Tuesdays at the same bench load, complete all printed flat and paused reps with at least three clean reps left on the final flat set both times. Move up one row on the next regular Tuesday. After a miss or all-out set, move down one row. Saturday follows Tuesday. On the heaviest row, add one rep at a time to the first flat set, while leaving at least two in reserve. Cap that set at eight reps in weeks 2–8 and six in weeks 10–16.

Weeks 12 and 16. Take one smooth practice single around RPE 7 (about three clean reps in reserve) before the work sets. Select the single independently from the work weight using your log. If a warm-up already reaches that effort, count it and end the ramp. This single does not qualify you for a load increase. The Normal examples, in squat / bench / sumo order, are 120 / 85 / 150 kg in week 12 and 125 / 90 / 155 kg in week 16. The mid-block sets of four below are separate training checkpoints.

Weeks 9 and 17. The easier main sessions use 95 / 70 / 110 kg and 100 / 70 / 120 kg respectively, in squat / bench / sumo order. Allowing recovery here helps the next block start from a repeatable baseline.

Weeks 18–23. Perform one working single. Earn the next 5 kg after two comparable main sessions at the same weight on different weeks: single at RPE 7.5 or easier, all back-off reps complete, and at least two clean reps left on the last set both times. Repeat at RPE 8–8.5. At RPE 9 or above, reduce the next heavy-session load by 5 kg. Choose a lighter single today if warm-ups feel unusually heavy. Weeks 18–19 use 85 or 90 kg when earned. Weeks 20–21 can reach 90 or 95 kg, and weeks 22–23 can reach 95 or 100 kg. Start at 80 kg if 85 is too heavy. Match back-offs and Saturday bench to the actual Tuesday single in the daily load options.

Week 24. Rehearse one familiar opener around RPE 7–8 and complete the shorter back-offs. Choose a weight you have already handled at that effort. A harder-than-expected warm-up can become today’s single. Record the decision for test week. Typical openers are squat 130–135, bench 90 and sumo 160–165 kg. Use a lighter row where indicated by your log.

Week 25. Taper with 105 kg squat for 2 × 2, 80 kg bench for 3 × 2 and 130 kg sumo for 2 × 2. Keep Saturday bench and dips easy.

Treat these sets as signs of readiness. Log actual RPE and technique. If the late marker is still close to maximal, choose the lighter test route. If heavier evidence is already repeatable, consider the stretch route.

Week 26. Test on separate days. The normal routes are squat 130 → 140 → 145 kg, bench 90 → 95 → 100 kg and sumo 160 → 170 → 175 kg. Rest 4–6 minutes. The daily cards print lighter and stretch attempts, the 5 kg alternative to a lower-body opening jump, and the effort that warrants another attempt. A bench single at 90 or 95 kg at a genuine RPE 9 ends that day’s test.

When progress slows. If three comparable main sessions stall or regress, review the training log, sleep, food, bodyweight, technique and secondary work before raising the load. For lower-body fatigue, reduce Monday’s two paused-sumo sets to one easy set or omit Friday’s optional back extension. For pressing, compare Tuesday bench and Saturday bench and dips at similar bodyweight, load and effort. If fatigue from Thursday persists over two comparable Saturdays, use one incline set until performance recovers.

Choosing weights at your gym

Barbells move in 5 kg steps · Belts move in 2.5 kg steps

Gym increments. All barbell numbers include the 20 kg bar. The smallest total change is 5 kg with one 2.5 kg plate on each side. A dip or pull-up belt can move in 2.5 kg steps.

Read the calendar with your log. Normal is a worked example. Lighter and Heavier show two alternatives for that week. Keep an earned weight across weeks if needed. Open “All load options and warm-ups” on the main day when the closest example differs from your working weight. Warm-up numbers are kg × reps. Log each work set, the effort on the last set and each working single.

Starting an existing calendar. Use your most recent comparable sessions to choose your entry weight. Keep your current dates and completed entries. When changing phase, start at the lower end if recent performance calls for it. Reps in reserve (RIR) are additional clean reps you judge you could perform. RPE 7, 8 and 9 correspond roughly to three, two and one rep in reserve.

Secondary barbell work. Use the Friday squat or Monday paused-sumo example matching the main lift’s row. If holding a weight outside the three examples, use the closest secondary row and keep it easy. Monday’s two paused-sumo sets finish with at least three clean reps left. Taper and test weeks omit them.

Belt lifts. Begin with a row you can complete at the printed effort. Move up a belt row after two comparable sessions with four or more clean reps left on the final set. In week 1, one such session is enough. Use the lighter row if you miss the targets or have less than one rep left. Log bodyweight alongside the added load. A useful dip milestone is +25 kg for three clean sets of six at a consistent depth.

Low-incline bench. Follow the reps and weights in its own Weights tab. The Thursday session shows the current phase prescription.

Bodyweight work and low-incline bench

Thursday pressing and the four bodyweight benchmarks

  • Weeks 2–8, 10–16 and 18–23. Do pull-ups, strict push-ups and then 30° low-incline barbell bench for 2 × 8–12. On the bodyweight sets, follow the printed target and stop when one clean rep remains. Leave two on incline.
  • Weeks 24 and 25. Use one set of eight on incline. Leave at least three reps in week 24 and four in week 25.
  • Benchmark dates. In weeks 1, 9 and 17, test on Thursday. In week 26, test on Saturday after the three barbell tests. Do one strict set each of pull-ups, dips and push-ups in that order, five minutes apart. Record clean reps and bodyweight so the four tests are comparable.
  • Other accessories. Choose a weight that leaves the printed number of clean reps. When every set reaches the top of the range at that effort, move up one available increment.

Standards

The same on every rep.

  • Squat: hip crease at or below the top of the knee, lower back neutral. Raise your heels slightly if you need to.
  • Bench: touch the chest, no bounce, same grip; safeties set or a spotter.
  • Sumo: reset every rep on the floor, brace, push the floor away, stand tall without leaning back.
  • Pull-ups and dips: dead hang to chin over the bar; dips to the same depth every rep with full lockout.
  • Push-ups: chest to a fist-height marker, full lockout, body in one line.

Mobility

Before squat and sumo days, and after every session.

Before squat and sumo days

  • 3 minutes of easy cycling or walking.
  • Knee-to-wall ankle rocks, 2 × 10 per side. Adductor rock-backs, 10.
  • Goblet squat hold at your depth, 2 × 20–30 s. Hip-hinge drill, 8.

After every session, 6–8 minutes

  • Every day: hamstring stretch lying on your back with a strap, 2 × 45 s per side.
  • Mon, Wed, Fri: half-kneeling hip flexor 2 × 30 s per side; frog inner-thigh stretch 2 × 45 s; wall calf stretch 2 × 30 s per side.
  • Tue, Thu, Sat: 90/90 hip switches × 8; figure-4 glute stretch 2 × 30 s per side.

Check every 4 weeks

  • Weeks 1, 5, 9, 13, 17, 21 and 26: fingertip-to-floor distance, knee-to-wall distance per side, and a side-on squat video.

Back care

For your L5–S1 history.

You report an L5–S1 disc bulge about three years ago and are currently largely symptom-free. These rules keep the plan sensible for that history; they are not a diagnosis or clearance.

  • Squat to the deepest point you can reach with your lower back neutral.
  • If ordinary back fatigue accumulates, reduce Monday’s two paused-sumo sets to one easy set or omit Friday’s optional back extension. Reassess how Wednesday’s main pulls feel.
  • New pain spreading down a leg, tingling, numbness or weakness: stop the exercise that provokes it and see a clinician.

Get urgent care

  • New bladder or bowel problems, numbness around the groin or inner thighs, or leg weakness that is quickly getting worse.

Food and recovery

Eat to gain, sleep to recover.

  • Protein. Your reported intake is about 80 g daily. Aim first for around 110 g, about 30 g more per day. The plan’s working range is 110–120 g, spread across 3–5 meals.
  • Bodyweight: aim to gain 0.15–0.3 kg a week, roughly 4–7 kg by May. Start by adding about 300 calories a day; weigh yourself 3–4 mornings a week and adjust every 3 weeks by 150–200 calories.
  • Creatine (optional): 3–5 g of creatine monohydrate a day; expect 1–2 kg of early water weight.
  • Sleep: keep your 7–9 hours. Alcohol: not the night before Monday or Wednesday.

Chest and shoulders. During regular training, two low-incline sets give the upper chest a dedicated pressing angle. The front delts and triceps assist the presses. The build week also has six direct cable lateral-raise sets, three reverse pec-deck sets and two overhead cable triceps-extension sets. Rows provide additional rear-delt work. A compound set can involve several muscles.

Bench and weighted dips. Bench still comes first on Tuesday and Saturday. Saturday’s belt-loaded dips follow volume bench. The swap takes total weekly dip sets from five to three in a build week and retains all three weighted sets. Record bodyweight, added weight, set reps and effort under the same conditions. One weekly dip practice gives you fewer chances to practise high-rep dips.

Fatigue. The old final dip set left about one rep. Incline leaves two, and strict push-ups now come first among Thursday’s presses. Push-up reps and soreness can change even with the same set count. If two comparable Saturdays show fewer bench or weighted-dip reps at similar load and effort, and Thursday fatigue is lingering, use one incline set with three reps left next training Thursday. Resume two sets once performance and recovery settle.

Lower-body recovery. Monday has two easy paused-sumo sets before Wednesday’s main pull. Friday’s squat stays secondary. Check whether either exposure affects the next main session. Test-week sumo moves to Thursday and bodyweight tests to Saturday.

Practice weeks. Weeks 12 and 16 each add one low-fatigue bench single near RPE 7. The other 16 pressing sets match an ordinary build or strength week. A single provides practice with a heavier weight and contributes less repeated-rep work than an incline or dip set.

Evidence behind the design

What the structure leans on.

  • A small training study in untrained men found greater upper-site chest growth with incline-only pressing. The mixed and flat-only groups had similar results. Chaves et al., 2020.
  • Heavier resistance training across at least two weekly sessions supports maximal strength. The bench targets and thresholds here are individual coaching choices. ACSM position stand, 2026.
  • Varying load and reps within the week favoured strength in trained lifters: Moesgaard et al., 2022.
  • Muscle growth improves as sets end closer to failure; strength gains are similar across a wide range of reps in reserve: Robinson et al., 2024.
  • Each muscle at least twice a week, heavy loads for strength, about 10 sets per muscle per week for growth: ACSM position stand, 2026.
  • Full squats grew the glutes and inner thighs more than half squats: Kubo et al., 2019, summary.
  • Strength training improves range of motion about as much as stretching: Alizadeh et al., 2023; at least 5 minutes of stretching per muscle per week: Thomas et al., 2018.
  • Seated leg curls grew the hamstrings more than lying ones, 14% against 9%: Maeo et al., 2021.
  • Standing calf raises grew the calves far more than seated ones: Kinoshita et al., 2023.
  • Machines and free weights built similar muscle, while strength improved most on the tool that was trained: Haugen et al., 2023.
  • Overhead triceps extensions grew the triceps more than pushdowns, the long head most of all (28.5% against 19.6%): Maeo et al., 2023, summary.
  • Bench pressing alone barely grew the triceps long head; adding triceps extensions did: Brandão et al., 2020, summary.
  • Weight gain and protein targets for intermediate lifters: Iraki et al., 2019. Creatine safety: ISSN position stand.

Training weights

Choose a lift and row for all 26 weeks. Rows are examples. Use the daily load options if your log calls for another weight. Thursday incline follows your rep log.

Choose your squat weights

Reported best 125 kg · Goal 145 kg · Stretch 150 kg

Build and strength. Working options are 100, 105, 110, 115 kg for 4 × 5–6 in weeks 2–8, followed by 110, 115, 120, 125 kg for 4 × 3–4 in weeks 10–16. Use the row your log supports. Start a new weight at the bottom of the rep range. Move up 5 kg on a future main day only after two comparable sessions at the same weight reach the top on every set, with at least two clean reps left on the final set both times. With one left, repeat. After a miss or a grind, use the next lighter load. Easier weeks and secondary work do not count.

Weeks 12 and 16. The Normal practice singles are 120 and 125 kg, respectively. Aim for RPE 7. Choose these independently from the work sets.

Heavy weeks. Perform one working single. Earn the next 5 kg after two comparable main sessions at the same weight on different weeks: single at RPE 7.5 or easier, all back-off reps complete, and at least two clean reps left on the last set both times. Repeat at RPE 8–8.5. At RPE 9 or above, reduce the next heavy-session load by 5 kg. Choose a lighter single today if warm-ups feel unusually heavy. Week 24 rehearses a familiar opener, usually 130 or 135 kg at RPE 7–8. Week 25 reduces work.

Readiness. 120 kg × 4 around RPE 8 in the strength block and 135 kg × 1 around RPE 8 late in the program support considering the goal route.

Monday test. Use up to three singles and rest 4–6 minutes between attempts. Choose an opener recently completed cleanly at RPE 8 or easier. If warm-ups feel heavy, use the lighter route. For 130 → 140 → 145 kg, take the 10 kg first increase only if 130 is clearly comfortable (about RPE 7–7.5 or easier) and your recent work supports 140. Otherwise, if another attempt is warranted, use 130 → 135 → 140 kg. Take the final 5 kg only after a clean second with clear reserve. The stretch route requires a recently repeatable opener. End the session after a miss, a grind, loss of your usual technique or concerning symptoms. Log the heaviest completed single.

Choose your Thursday weight

30° low-incline barbell bench · 20 kg bar · 5 kg total steps

Start with a 45 kg trial after warming up. Use 40 kg if necessary for eight reps with two left. Keep the same weight until you can do 12 + 12 with at least two clean reps left. Then move one step to the right for your next regular session and aim for eight per set.

20 · 25 · 30 · 35 · 40 · 45 · 50 · 55 · 60 · 65 · 70 kg. Continue in 5 kg steps. To reduce the load, move one step to the left. Stay at or above the 20 kg empty bar.

A jump from 45 to 50 kg means adding one 2.5 kg plate to each side. If you can use 50 kg for the first eight only, use 45 kg for the second set and log both. After a benchmark Thursday, resume from the last regular session. A reduced rehearsal-week or taper set earns no load increase.
